ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u84	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. Chairman. Dear Minister, co-worker of the Ministry, distinguished colleagues and colleagues. The unanimous decision of the State Council of the Republic of Slovenia on the vote, on the veto voted on by the law on the elimination of the consequences of asbestos work, allows all to reconsider the content of the proposed law and offers us the opportunity to pass a re-examination test so that, on the basis of the professional arguments already presented and the warnings of the interested and other public of the inappropriate law, we do not confirm. At the beginning of the presentation of the position of the LDS Club on the request of the Council of State to re-decision on the law on the elimination of the consequences of asbestos work, we would like to remind citizens and citizens and the government once again of the fact that large amounts of asbestos still exist in our environment, which pose a potential threat to each of us. In the Western world, asbestos, which is a common name for a group of fibre silicons, is considered to be a miracle ore with versatile utility. It is therefore present in more than 3,000 products. The most famous such products are water and sewage pipes, roof coverings made of asbestos cement, type of insulation for buildings and electrical guides, brake discs and seals, and longer asbestos fibres were also used in the textile industry. Even in Slovenia, a third of buildings are still covered with critiques containing solidly bound asbestos, asbestos water pipes are still over 1,000 kilometers away, and many buildings, although the data is not clear, have insulation, interference or soil from materials with weak asbestos. Between 1946 and 1996, while 67,000 tonnes of asbestos were imported into Slovenia, 14 companies used it in their production, 10 companies incorporated it into imported products and 8 companies sold it in retail. A year ago, at the LDS of the current Minister for the Environment and Space of the Member State, we drew attention to this public often with little known fact and problem. We also asked him how the competent inspection oversees the implementation of the regulations governing asbestos management and whether specific financial incentives should be introduced for the professional replacement of construction materials containing hazardous asbestos. These issues are particularly important in the light of international research, which points out that asbestos users are even more at risk than workers in asbestos and asbestos production itself. Unfortunately, the Minister for Environment and Space's response was not very encouraging. According to the assessment of the Ministry of Environment and Space, about 400,000 tonnes of asbestos-cement coverage will have to be replaced and stored safely in our country by 2020. That quantity does not, of course, include other wastes which also contain asbestos. According to the Minister, the number of inspections of the environment and nature inspection despite the fact that the amount of asbestos waste is increasing significantly from year to year. This information must be of concern to us, as well as the information provided by the Minister, that this government is probably not planning to provide specific financial incentives for the professional replacement of construction materials containing hazardous asbestos. That this area is almost completely unsupervised, only 9 inspections in 2004 have been carried out, while it is noted that several cases have been identified where the removal of asbestos-cement coverings on a surface of 1,000 square metres was carried out by operators who did not have any authority to do such work, which poses a great threat to all those exposed to asbestos dust. In doing so, I would like to point out that even after one year, the Minister of Environment and Space has not yet fulfilled the promise that the Ministry of the Environment and Space will issue a special package to inform citizens and citizens of the risks associated with asbestos, the way in which roof coverings from asbestos and the way in which these waste is disposed of and other asbestos products are disposed of. Obviously, the government is unaware of the seriousness of this issue, which unfortunately is also reflected in the case of the law that we will be deciding again today. Most of the tragic consequences of the mass use of asbestos could have been avoided in the world and could have been avoided by taking into account more than 100 years old warnings about the harmful effects of asbestos on human health. Unfortunately, these early warnings were not given greater attention. The measures to limit and prohibit the use of asbestos occurred only after the discovery of the severe consequences of exposure to asbestos dust and many tragic losses of human life in the late 1980s. Only in 1998 and 1999 did the European Union countries definitively prohibit the use of all types and forms of asbestos. In 2001, however, the dispute between the World Trade Organisation, which rejected the Canadian complaint and supported the European ban, also ended. So, over a century after the first warnings of asbestos damage. In our country, too, and compared to some other countries, in 1996 we banned the production and transport of asbestos products by special law, arranged for the restructuring of asbestos production into asbestos and resolved some of the social issues of workers who were exposed to the harmful effects of asbestos dust. Despite the fact that it was adopted too late, this law plays a central role in solving the asbestos problem in our country. When the restructuring of asbestos production into non-terrestrial production ended in 2003, it became apparent that it was necessary to upgrade the law and provide solutions in the long term in the interest of people who were or will be affected by exposure to the harmful effects of asbestos dust. The former Government, led by the LDS, was aware of this fact, and in early 2004, it established a special interdepartmental working group, which prepared a comprehensive review of the implementation of the existing law, a forecast of the assessment of patients and a starting point for the drafting of a new law. As a result of the end of the term of office, the previous government failed to prepare, which is why the current government has taken over the task. In January 2005, we extended the 1996 law in the National Assembly. After the re-renewal was rejected, the government finally submitted a proposal for a new law on the removal of the consequences of asbestos work to the legislative process. Despite the fact that the government had already prepared professional grounds for the new law and that more than enough time had been taken to prepare the marriage text, it turned out that it had not been aligned at all with those interested in it, i.e. people who, unfortunately, also pay for centuries of error in their lives. There was simply no necessary dialogue between the government and affected citizens and citizens. This is also the first observation we and still refer to the government at the LDS. Even more so, it will be in the eyes and arouses concern about the lack of cooperation of the government or the lawmakers for the medical profession. As a result of such behaviour, a number of comments were received by the social partners, companies, professional associations and the association and society of asbestos patients. Despite the fact that, after extensive discussion at the home work body, a complementary draft law containing some minor amendments to the legal text was prepared, the LDS felt that they were not correcting the legal text in order to ensure equal rights for all people who were or will still be affected by exposure to the harmful effects of asbestos. Therefore, together with the Group of Social Democrats and the Member of the Srečko Friends of the National Assembly, we have proposed an amendment based on the fact that Slovenia is also a social state and the principle of justice and solidarity. The ruling coalition has rejected all proposed amendments. For the sake of understanding and re-explaining the public, I would like to reiterate what solutions we have proposed with these amendments, because the fact is that the amendments put the text of the law in a different context. In particular, we have proposed that, on more favourable terms, all persons who have been identified as professional or other diseases due to exposure to asbestos be entitled to compensation and pension. So also the inhabitants who lived in the business premises of companies where asbestos or asbestos products were processed or stored. We have suggested that, under certain conditions, legal heirs of pensions are also entitled to compensation, which is unfortunately still compulsory children, since all too often the beneficiaries of lung cancer are dying at the age of about 35. Furthermore, we have proposed that compensation of 60% of the amount fixed should be covered by the Republic of Slovenia in respect of damages in judicial proceedings, and that in cases of cancer, compensation should be covered in full. We are convinced that the Republic of Slovenia cannot and must not deny the responsibility for the presence of a large amount of asbestos in our environment, which can be carcinogenised by the minimal inhalation of their fibres. We have also proposed that the fifth member of the Interdepartmental Commission be proposed by organisations of persons affected by asbestos. In our view, it is a civilisation standard for patients to be able to participate in decision-making on the rights of the sick. Last but not least, with the proposed amendments, we opposed the tightening of the conditions for obtaining a disability pension under more favourable conditions. We therefore proposed that the right to early retirement should be exclusively linked to the disease found and not to the additional conditions that the 1996 law did not contain. Your Honor! If the ruling coalition of the proposed amendments were supported at the meeting of the National Assembly in the second reading, the requirements of the Council of State for re-decision on this law would most likely not be supported. The Council of State has therefore given the ruling coalition time to reconsider whether it actually wants to share with the law the same diseases due to exposure to asbestos, namely those who would have rights under this law, and those who would not have these rights. The National Council has also allowed the ruling coalition to decide again whether it really wants to reduce the rights already acquired by the 1996 law. In order to facilitate the decision, I would point out the results of the London hospital, which are based on data collected between 1917 and 1964, that more than 50% of the 96 cases studied for mesothelioma were exposed to asbestos professionally or privately. As an example, it is the patients who lived with a worker who had contact with asbestos. One third of the rest lived in areas less than 800 metres from the asbestos factory. While part of the ruling coalition predicts an immediate amendment to this moment of the unadopted law on the elimination of the consequences of asbestos work. In the LDS, if such conduct is indeed to occur and the law is to be reconfirmed today, we consider it to be an unrealistic and irresponsible act of the ruling coalition, which puts its political prestige ahead of many human destinys, the loss of life and the immeasurable suffering of individuals and their relatives. Your Honor! We will not support the law on the removal of the consequences of working with asbestos in re-decision in the LDS. All who suffer or suffer from asbestos deserve a more social and just marriage. At the same time, we hope and wish for the well-being of all those who will suffer in any way the consequences of asbestos exposure, so that most Members and Members will follow the decision of the parent working body, which did not provide the necessary support for the re-decision of the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u86	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. A respected choir! In the Group of Social Democrats, we believe that this law is a step backwards in solving the asbestos problem in Slovenia, as its title narrows down the content of the law itself. The law applies only to the elimination of the consequences of asbestos work, but not to the harmful effects of exposure to hazardous asbestos. It therefore applies only to workers employed in asbestos production, but not to residents exposed to asbestos in the immediate vicinity of production facilities. In the Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats, we will take into account the veto of the State Council of the Republic of Slovenia and vote against the law. Slovenia has been facing harmful effects of asbestos dust in production and around production facilities for at least 35 years, since the 1970s. Asbestos was used in Slovenia, for example in Maribor, from the second half of the 19th century and in the Soča Valley from 1921 onwards. In the last three decades, the medical profession and some companies have begun to draw attention to these problems, especially Salonit Anhovo. At the level of the country, however, a law was adopted in 1996 prohibiting the production and transport of asbestos products and regulating the restructuring of asbestos production to inequity and also regulating the social rights of employees. This law was unanimously adopted by Members and Members from all parliamentary groups in the National Assembly. The new law proposed by the government is, in our view, a step back in this area. That we do not take asbestos seriously enough in our country, we also bear witness that several years ago in Slovenia we discovered a new focus of asbestos patients, in Slovenian railways, in a company that is 100% owned by the country. Last December, the company sent 100 workers to the hospital in Golnik for specialist examinations, the results of which will be known at the end of this month. However, these survey workers have said that, after asserting the management of the Slovenian Railways, they will have to work with asbestos for another 10 years, even though the legal ban on the use of asbestos in production has been in force in our country for 10 years. Unfortunately, all the opposition's additions were rejected. As early as December 2004, the Interdepartmental Government Working Group noted that in the group of beneficiaries of compensation, I quote, "all potential exhibitors, employees or former employees in the production process or service services who have been directly or indirectly in contact with asbestos dust, as well as residents due to environmental exposure to asbestos through the diagnosis of asbestos disease - mesothelioma." From 1964 to 2002, 365 mesotheliomas were discovered in Slovenia, of which 95% were on the 5km radio around asbestos factories. One third of the mesothelioms fall off on Maribor, because there was a track-vehicle factory where pure asbestos was used. Sadly, the majority of the patients did not realize that they had died from asbestos. Asbestos exposed workers in the shipyard, who now live and get sick in neighbouring Croatia. In the last 10 years, diseases have also occurred in the gastro-intestinal pool. All certified occupational asbestos diseases, however, have so far been around 1,700 among workers. In the event that the settlement or action proceedings cannot be brought to an end, it would be necessary to continue to communicate with the affected heirs, but only if he has already filed a claim for compensation for the period of life to which he is entitled, but because of the long-term judicial proceedings he has not received. Asbestos alone was exposed to about 23,000 employees in Slovenia. The peak of asbestos disease, due to the long latent period until the outbreak of the disease, is expected by the medical profession around 2025. Forecasts for the EU over the next 35 years speak of 250,000 dead and, according to the worst forecast, 400,000. Your Honor! ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u87	Thank you very much. And the last one, mag. Capuder on behalf of New Slovenia.
ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u88	Thank you very much, Mr. Chairman. Messengers and Members, dear guests! The law on the elimination of the consequences of asbestos work, which we reaffirm in the National Assembly, regulates the identification of occupational and other diseases and the reduction of life functions due to exposure to asbestos dust, the recognition of lump sum compensation for diseases and the provision of the right to a pension under more favourable conditions. Beneficiaries under this Act will all those who have been employed in companies that have processed, used, stored, implanted or removed asbestos or asbestos products, but in jobs where they were exposed to asbestos and have become sick. By means of legal solutions, we are expanding the range of beneficiaries who have come into contact with asbestos in their workplace, according to the old law banning production and trafficking in asbestos products. And because of this expansion, the marriage is good and better than it is now valid. I am convinced that the proposed law is also good to solve problems for railway workers or their central facilities, where a new large range of asbestos diseases has recently been discovered. On Slovenian railways, for 10 years after the legal regulation of the asbestos problem, it has not yet been resolved or has only begun to resolve itself. The new law will give workers rights that they have never had before. I'm sure of it. Therefore, in New Slovenia we cannot understand the decision of the State Council to veto this law. Our view is that compensation must be granted to all workers who have worked in contact with asbestos and have become sick. The veto of the State Council only delays the exercise of a right that has been owned by workers since 1996, but has not been properly regulated. We must also be aware of this in the National Assembly when we vote on this law again today. The right to go to sick workers for asbestos must not be denied, no matter where they were employed. For this reason, the law on the elimination of the consequences of working with asbestos needs to be re-examined. I would also point out that the 1996 law on the prohibition of production and transport of asbestos provided for only employees in establishments and that no person who had not been employed in production establishments had obtained rights under this law. This is what the letter from the country’s asbestos production plant, which I presented at the previous meeting of the National Assembly when we adopted the law, also speaks of. Therefore, the new law does not in any way violate existing rights. The right to a disability pension under the new law will be enforceable by all beneficiaries if, under the general rules on pension and disability insurance, disability has been established as a result of a occupational disease under this law and if they have completed 25 years of retirement and are at least 50 years of age. The proposal sets out the maximum amounts of compensation for occupational diseases due to exposure to asbestos. It is proposed that the country covers at least 60% and that the company has a maximum of 40% of the compensation costs per disease. The amount of compensation for recognised diseases under this law will range from 2.5 million to 10 million tolars, with clear criteria and ranges being clearly defined in the new law, which is better from now on. In some countries of the European Union, asbestos workers do not receive compensation for lighter forms of disease. This is the case in Britain, France, the Netherlands, Germany. In Slovenia, therefore, we have taken a major step forward in protecting the rights of workers who have come into contact with asbestos and have therefore become sick. Slovenia will thus be one of the few countries that allows for the right to compensation for better forms of asbestos disease. In our view, the bill, which we reaffirm, has the right solutions both for those who are sick and for those who are or will still be in a difficult situation due to asbestos diseases. In Novi Slovenija we are convinced that it is primarily for them, i.e. workers, to allocate additional resources if any of the recognised forms of disease are identified. This will be identified by an interdisciplinary group of experts appointed by law by the Minister responsible for health. In view of the above, the law on the elimination of the consequences of asbestos is better than the law in force so far and is, in our view, quite comparable to similar European laws. On the other hand, we are also aware that this law does not solve the problem of all those who have suffered from asbestos in the environment, particularly in the area of large production plants. Above all, there is a problematic definition of the environment, because if we define an area in the law, then we can do wrong to the one who will suffer from asbestos disease and will live outside this area and will not be entitled to compensation under law. However, the spread across Slovenia and the solution in the manner envisaged in the adopted law would likely represent financial unforeseeable effects. The new Slovenia will propose and also ensure that the solution to this problem continues. We are also aware of other asbestos issues, such as the replacement of asbestos pipelines and roofs, which must be addressed by another law, not by the proposed one. However, it is now important that we first tackle the rights of those most exposed. These are the workers. Therefore, in Nova Slovenija, we will once again support the law on the elimination of the consequences of asbestos work. Thank you very much.

In Semich, where the production of capacitors was carried out with the so-called PCB, we still have an environment with this substance contaminated, as well as water, and we have proven there also a number of people who have received cancer as a result of these substances, and none of these people have received any compensation or any conditions for early retirement or retirement under more favourable conditions. Today we also use a number of chemical resources for agriculture, starch. We know that in a few years we will be able to witness again to the conclusion that all these substances cause many diseases, including incurable diseases. Today we know that passive smoking also causes cancer, causes many diseases, and unfortunately we have not taken any more important steps to date in this area. More than that, we still use a lot of this substance today. Now we must certainly ask ourselves who is responsible for all these substances that are environmentally harmful or affect human health. Is only the country responsible or are the producers responsible, or are they users, or are we all, last but not least? I agree with the fact that the State is certainly the first one to be concerned about preventing the use of harmful substances, but can only interfere when the harmfulness of the substance is professionally justified. I agree that the state is then trying to ease the difficulties, but there is no way that it can prevent or restore things to their original state. Here I am thinking, above all, that a sick person cannot be healed with any such great compensation. And here to impose some high compensation on the state for such cases, I think it is irresponsible. The law that we are considering today is one of such laws that try to mitigate the consequences of working with asbestos within those frameworks that our society can still bear. However, I find it difficult to imagine the consequences for our society, for Slovenia, if this law tried to involve all people who have become sick or will suffer from asbestos for two reasons. The first is certainly that the financial burden would be unsustainable, both for us and for our later generations. However, another aspect is that this law would then be incorrect to those who have also suffered from the harmful effects of the environment or harmful substances at work as well as outside work. As far as I am aware, we do not yet have any law in Slovenia that would thus regulate the disease for some other consequences of environmental or material effects. And none of these have been compensated. Here I would only touch, as I mentioned earlier, the consequences of the harmfulness of secondhand smoking. On Saturday, I saw not a comment in one of the Slovenian newspapers, but an article stating that 303 people died in Slovenia in 2002 as a result of second-hand smoking. There are 79,000 such cases in the European Union. I'm sure you all agree with me that even in Slovenia we have not done everything we can to prevent smoking in public spaces or wherever people do not smoke. For me, this number is frightening, and I hope that we will do something about it. 303 persons who died in 2002 were more than suffering from asbestos, and at least according to the data I have, no compensation was paid to all of these 303 people, and there were also no claims for this or the possibility that people who had also suffered from second-hand smoking could retire under favourable conditions. I will support the asbestos law in the proposed form because it is either in an corrective way or in an optimal way to resolve the situation we are facing in Slovenia. I agree that this is not a solution that would satisfy everything, but in such a form it is correct for all patients who have, in one way or another, fallen ill in Slovenia or will. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u128	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Dear government representative, honourable Members, honourable Members! The content of the law under the title of the law will therefore be the treatment of workers who have encountered asbestos in the production process and the provision of assistance in the broadest humane sense of assistance to these workers. The Council of State states, inter alia, that the current and still applicable so-called asbestos law already confers wider rights on the categories of residents living in the business premises of companies where asbestos or asbestos products have been processed or stored. This claim is not true because the existing law actually confers rights only on workers who have been employed in the production, processing or storage of asbestos. So far, no person who has not been employed in companies where she has been professionally exposed to asbestos has been granted rights under the existing law. I therefore argue that the new asbestos law does not violate existing rights in this regard. On the contrary. The law of justice extends to all those who have processed asbestos that have incorporated asbestos materials that have removed asbestos materials or used them. The new law, I believe, regulates qualitatively, for the first time, occupational diseases related to asbestos, such as plaques, benign pleural effusion, chronic pleural effusion, asbestososis and lung cancer and mesothelioma. Secondly, the law regulates the assessment and payment of lump sum compensation from almost 12,000 euros to a good 41,000 euros, as at least 60% of the state pays, 40% of the company or state is more appropriate if the company is in poor condition or no longer. And the third time. The law regulates the right to a disability pension under more favourable conditions. ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u158	Thank you for your word. Nice greetings to Members of Parliament and Members and representatives of the Government. For several reasons, the Council of State decided to veto the Act on the Supplementation of the War Veterans Act and voted on it at its 12th extraordinary session on 9 March. Procedure for the adoption, excessive and unjustified extension of the circle of beneficiaries and the inconsistencies and inconsistencies of the provisions of the law and the termination of the law on the financing of the association of national liberation fighters. All these comments were also made by the Commission of the State Council for Social Activities at its 40th meeting, when it did not support the law. The National Assembly did not take these warnings into account when adopting the draft law. The amendment of the law is of such importance that it should be submitted and adopted on a regular basis with the possibility of a wider public debate. In the opinion of the Council of State, the law is too loose and indeterminate to extend the range of beneficiaries, reducing the importance and role of a war veteran and neglecting and valuing the values that would lead to the granting of the status of actual beneficiaries. There are now 39,961 war veterans in Slovenia for Slovenia. Councillors pointed out that, by unduly extending the rights by obtaining the status of the applicant, it would not only apply 4,400 persons at the discretion of the proposer, but significantly more. It is known, however, what rights go to all of them and what resources will be needed to do so. Too poorly defined in the law is the issuing of certificates for the participation of records to demonstrate the credibility of cooperation and the identification of what someone had to do to gain status. Councillors were of the opinion that the rights were being extended to employees and workers too loosely, for example, all directors were then responsible for defence preparations and the Council of State proposes that the list of beneficiaries be re-examined. With the implementation of this law, the law on the financing of associations for national liberation fighters ceases to apply. This is unacceptable to the State Council for several reasons. It should be noted that the fighters of the national liberation war of Slovenia have a special position and special protection provided by the constitutional law for the implementation of the fundamental constitutional charter of the independence and independence of the Republic of Slovenia. Article 18 states that the Republic of Slovenia shall ensure the protection of the rights of fighters to the extent and under the conditions laid down by the provisions of the SFRJ pending the implementation of this Act. Therefore, there is neither a legal basis nor a substantive argument to intervene in these rights. The criteria and criteria for financing the activities of war veterans should, according to the State Council, be defined in the law itself. The adoption of the law will allow the competent minister to abolish the financing of certain activities of the National Liberation War Associations, as the determination of the criteria will be a free judgement. The Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly has also pointed out that it would raise the issue of the specific situation and protection of fighters, since it is established by constitutional law, to examine and clarify in particular. We are very keen to compare ourselves with Europe, and all Europe recognises the fighters against Nazism and the Fascism as a special status. The NOB fighters are also recognized as part of the victorious coalition of World War II because they fought for the liberation of the country in the fight against Nazism and Fascism. In Slovenia alone, the current government is inclined to recognise these special rights to those who have cooperated with the fascists and Nazis, while taking them to those to whom they are going. Therefore, the adoption of such legislation is just another attempt to evaluate the importance and role of national liberation fighters and their national liberation struggle. With all due respect to all war veterans, it is not possible to compare all veteran societies and all veterans. The difference is whether you're at war for 23 days or 4 years or you're fully equipped or naked and bare. For the sake of all this, the Council of State proposes to delete Article 9 of this Law. So many introductorys. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u161	Thank you very much. I give the text to the representative of the proposer of the law for explanation or for the opinion of the government. Mr. Petty Officer, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u162	Once again a lovely greeting, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The preparation of the proposal, which you will vote on today, started on the basis of the initiative of the Federation of Veterans of the War on Slovenia in May 2004. The Union also proposed the inclusion of the so-called civil part of the defence of citizens of the Republic of Slovenia who actively participated in the preparation and implementation of defence tasks during the aggression against Slovenia, based on the work obligations and decisions of the competent republic, provincial or municipal authorities and commanders. Individual associations of war veterans have also proposed to adjust the single financing of veteran organisations from the budget of the Republic of Slovenia. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia has prepared a supplement to the law on the basis of initiatives and experience in the implementation of the law. This proposal removes the criminal provisions of the current law, which excluded certain categories of persons who indisputably carried out defence tasks from the possibility of obtaining military veteran status. The law establishes uniform criteria and criteria for the allocation of budgetary resources to veteran societies or their associations acting as public-interest societies. There is no legal and non-personal reason for treating veteran organisations differently, so that veterans would be in a worse position for Slovenia than veterans, NOB fighters. By repealing the law on the financing of the NOB Association of Fighters, the current means of providing funds for its operation will not change. That law is no longer in line with the legal order in force, since it was adopted at a time when the union of fighters was one of the three social political organisations with special constitutional powers. In accordance with the new Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, the association of NOB fighters was transformed into a social organization in 1991 after the then applicable law on societies. In 1996, it was granted the status of a society acting in the public interest, in particular in the field of socio-health care for membership, preservation of historical tradition and the like. In view of the above, it is appropriate that the association of associations of fighters be subject to the same criteria for financing its activities as other companies and associations of war veterans granted the status of a company operating in the public interest. Even more budgetary resources are provided in 2006 for the financing of NOB societies or associations than for veteran wars for Slovenia, although there are several veterans of the war for Slovenia. Also, the association of associations of fighters has real estate that other veteran organizations do not have. The claims that this law violates Article 18 of the Constitutional Act for the implementation of a basic constitutional instrument which guarantees NOB fighters the same rights as those laid down by the SFRY rules are unfounded and do not hold back legal judgment. It is clear that this provision applies exclusively to the protection of individual rights of fighters, but not to the status of jurisdiction and the way in which organisations of fighters have no connection with the aforementioned provision. Individual rights of fighters shall be established by other regulations which are not affected by this proposal. The constitutional law also applies exclusively to rights guaranteed under the rules of the former SFRJ and the law on the financing of associations of fighters is a 1990 copy of the Republic of Slovenia. The rights of fighters do not change with this law, so they remain the same. The proposed law therefore corrects the wrongs for obtaining the status of a war veteran and provides a uniform basis for equal status for all veteran organisations, so the government proposes that this law be supported by Members. Thank you very much.

The law should be introduced on a regular basis, as this is a very important content that should be brought to the attention of the general public. The second reason was that the number of beneficiaries is unwarrantedly expanding, reducing the importance and role of a war veteran and neglecting the values that would lead to the granting of this status to actual beneficiaries. And the last reason that is most important, according to the Council of State, is the termination of the law on the financing of the Association of Fighters of NOB Slovenia, since they will no longer have the resources provided for their operations. The proposed amendment to the war veterans law, which we are re-declaring today, is a relatively short law. On the surface, it's divided into three sets. The first is about financing societies or organisations of war veterans. The second set extends a series of persons who have the right to gain the status of a war veteran. The third set solves the problem of pension distribution to well-known war veterans and newly recognised beneficiaries. All of this will be recognised as a special period, which will be counted during their working hours. In Novi Slovenija we could even agree with the opinion of the State Council when they believe that after the amendment of this law there is too much justification for obtaining the status of war veteran for Slovenia in 1991. According to some estimates, there are around 5,000 beneficiaries, and it is mainly feared that the enlargement of beneficiaries under this law would devalue the importance of the veteran war on Slovenia. On the other hand, the circle of beneficiaries after this amendment of the law extends to those who have been released for any reason from the previous amendment of the law in 1999 and should have obtained this status according to the criteria set out in the current law. The law currently in force does not address quite a few categories of beneficiaries who, during the aggression against Slovenia, have been actively involved in the defence of the country with weapons in hand. To me, the most typical representatives of this forgetfulness are mainly prison guards and customs officers, who for unknown reasons have fallen out of the previous amendment of the law. The main committee reopened its debate under Article 9 of the Act, in so far as article 18 of the Constitutional Law has been violated by the implementation of the basic constitutional charter, which gives the fighter a special position or special protection. On this point, I must make it clear that the Government's legislative service has made a request for the termination of the law on the financing of associations of fighters. In Novi Slovenija we agree with the Government's position that the 18th Article of the Charter provides for the protection of the rights of NOB fighters at least to the extent guaranteed by the then regulations of the SFRJ. This right is also guaranteed by this law on war veterans, which does not change in these provisions. At this point, I must also clearly reject the position of the representative of the Council of State, who, on the Committee, expressed the view that all the time these governments have been faced with an unfriendly restrictive attitude towards the union of fighters and the complications with the financing of their organisation, but in particular they are disturbed by the very well-known and negative views of the competent minister and the political party from which the Minister comes. For these reasons, they are convinced in the Council of State that it would be inappropriate to set the criteria for financing a union of fighters, which would be a matter of the Minister's discretion. In New Slovenia, we are convinced that individuals who are veterans of the union of fighters will continue to have all the rights that have been recognised to them already. We are also convinced that all veterans of the union of fighters during the Ministry of Magi. The petty man was paid, or they took advantage of all the benefits they enjoyed under the law. However, the rights and duties of society or organisations will or should be equal to the rights of other societies to which the status of society or organisation is recognised in the public interest and this seems to us to be the right thing to do. Nor can the State Council's claim, which was also the main reason for the veto voted, that by the end of the law on the financing of the association of NOB Slovenije fighters, only these would no longer have the resources provided for their operation. They will receive funds under the same criteria as any other comparable society. The criteria for the allocation of funds are very clear and the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly was very clear on this point. In order for the Minister to be able to specify the criteria only on the basis and in accordance with the criteria laid down in Article 1 of the Act. Personally and also my colleagues in Novi Slovenija do not see any need for the organization of NOB fighters to have a different and more privileged status against other companies with recognised status of society in the public interest. We agree and support the solution in Article 1 of this Law to take into account, in particular, the programme of the society or association of war veterans, which ensures public interest. The second criterion is the number of members of war veterans and property owned by the company or association of war veterans. In New Slovenia, we will once again support the amendment of the war veterans law. Thank you very much.

Mr. Bevk, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2006-03-28-SDZ4-Redna-15.ana.u397	Thank you very much, Mr. President, for your word. A respected choir! According to the Socialist Group of Members, the law on the elimination of the consequences of working with asbestos is a step back in solving the asbestos problem in Slovenia, as its title narrows down the content of the law itself. The law applies only to the elimination of the consequences of asbestos work, but not to the harmful effects of exposure to hazardous asbestos. It therefore applies only to workers employed in asbestos production, but not to residents exposed to asbestos in the immediate vicinity of production facilities. I must point out that the law was adopted without the cooperation of the social partners and the medical profession, and the claims of the people who were exposed to the harmful effects of asbestos powder were also justified. With the proposed amendments, the Social Democrats wanted to complement the basic concept of the government asbestos law with an environmental and not just professional aspect of asbestos diseases, together with colleagues and colleagues from the opposition. Asbestos was exposed to about 23,000 employees in Slovenia. Experts estimate that the peak of disease in Slovenia will only be around 2025. Even more so are forecasts in the EU, as some 250,000 people are expected to die as a result of asbestos exposure and some 400,000 in the worst case scenarios. The Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats will vote against the law, calling on the government to prepare a new law that will comprehensively regulate asbestos issues in Slovenia and, besides professionally, implement the environmental aspect of the recognition of asbestos diseases. I also call on colleagues and colleagues from the coalition to reject the law, because the discipline of the rare coalition members who have opposed the law so far is not an honour of Slovenian parliamentary tradition. I would like to read the first paragraph of Article 82 of the Constitution before deciding. I quote: "The Members are representatives of all the people and are not bound to any instructions," and I add - not even from Gregorčič Street in Ljubljana. Thank you very much.
